# Parity research: feature inventories (2026-10-23)

Eight inventories made by readers over the cloned upstream repositories (`srl-labs/containerlab-app` at `165f7e1`, the
older `srl-labs/vscode-containerlab` at `6df8e96`) and over this repository's guides and code. One row per user-visible
feature as the reader found it; evidence paths are relative to the respective repository. The gap matrix built from these
is [PARITY.md](PARITY.md). Readers summarise what they read; a row is evidence of a feature's existence, not a review of it.

## containerlab-app (srl-labs): user documentation inventory

Containerlab Apps is a GUI for containerlab delivered as three apps that share one React topology editor, explorer and lab tools: a VS Code extension (runs containerlab directly on the machine where VS Code runs, including WSL and Remote SSH), a web app (one Docker container, ghcr.io/srl-labs/containerlab-web, port 3001) and a desktop app (Electron-style package for Linux, macOS and Windows). The web and desktop apps never touch Docker; they sign in to clab-api-server (HTTPS 8090, Linux PAM accounts, clab_api and clab_admins groups, JWT sessions) on one or more lab hosts. A fourth surface, a browser sandbox on GitHub Pages, edits topologies with files kept in browser storage and cannot deploy. A lab is a .clab.yml topology plus a .clab.yml.annotations.json file for positions, icons, colors, groups, text, shapes and viewer settings. The feature set covers: visual and YAML editing with deep node, link, kind-specific, stage and inheritance editors; a context-sensitive Apply button with dry-run plan, lifecycle menu and Run with options; per-node shell, SSH, logs and start/stop/restart; Wireshark/Edgeshark capture; Linux tc link impairments; inspect, live interface counters and image management; layouts, node and link styles, annotation shapes and traffic-rate widgets; node templates with interface patterns; SVG, Grafana Flow panel bundle and Draw.io export; shared labs; multi-endpoint management; and an embeddable read-only viewer (Zensical Markdown fence, clab-topology custom element, iframe postMessage protocol, mountViewer) with a customization studio. This inventory comes only from the Markdown docs and README, so UI behaviour that the docs do not describe (the contents of the General and Management settings tabs, exact menu locations of some actions) is flagged as uncertain. Evidence paths are relative to the root of the cloned containerlab-app repository (scratchpad/upstream/containerlab-app); line numbers are from the files as read.

## containerlab-app: web app (apps/web) and public sandbox (apps/web-public)

apps/web and apps/web-public are thin hosts. Each is only an HTML shell plus a one-line import of @srl-labs/containerlab-standalone-runtime (web-main, terminal-main, wireshark-vnc-main). The visible UI lives in packages/clab-ui (@containerlab/clab-ui: workspace rail, explorer, topology canvas, editors, dialogs, settings, themes). The runtime package (packages/standalone-runtime) wires clab-ui to a StandaloneBackend transport. apps/web uses the real transport against its own Fastify BFF (packages/app-server, with DTOs in packages/app-contract). The BFF holds per-browser sessions and proxies to one or more clab-api-server endpoints over HTTP, SSE and WebSockets (labs, files, terminals, capture, images, netem). apps/web-public swaps the transport for an in-browser sandbox (apps/web-public/src/sandboxBackend.ts and sandboxTransport.ts). It stores files, node templates and icons in localStorage, runs the same topology editor session core, and turns every lifecycle, endpoint, event, repository and archive capability off. Web serves three pages: index.html (workspace), terminal.html (detached terminal) and wireshark.html (capture viewer). Stack: pnpm 12.4 workspace on Node 24, Vite 8.3 (rolldown build, @vitejs/plugin-react 6, postcss-import and autoprefixer), React 19.3 and TypeScript 7, with Zustand 5 stores. UI is MUI 9.4 (material, icons-material, x-charts 9.15) with Emotion 11. Editor and canvas libraries: @xyflow/react 12 (React Flow), Monaco 0.55 plus monaco-yaml, xterm 6 with fit addon, three 0.186 (rail logo), maplibre-gl 6 (Geo layout), d3-force, elkjs, markdown-it, highlight.js, DOMPurify and ajv. Server: Fastify 5 (cookie, cors, static, websocket), ws and undici. Tests: Playwright 1.63, node:test and oxlint. Feature entries below are from the actual source; all evidence paths are relative to the cloned repository root containerlab-app/. The workspace/clab-ui code also ships VS Code-only pieces (welcome page, standalone inspect and node-impairment webviews, settings webview, viewer embed) that the web hosts never mount. They are deliberately not listed as web features.

205 features.

| Id | Area | Feature | What it does | How reached | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## VS Code Containerlab extension (srl-labs.vscode-containerlab; monorepo app 0.27.0 and archived standalone repo 0.26.3)

The Containerlab VS Code extension is a Linux/WSL-only extension (VS Code ^1.105.1, activates on startup) that wraps the containerlab CLI and the Docker or Podman socket. Its single Activity Bar container holds one React/MUI webview 'Explorer' (Running Labs, Undeployed Local Labs, Help and Feedback) with per-row quick actions and grouped context menus, plus a hidden proxy tree view that supplies the running-lab badge. Everything else is a command (about 85 contributed, a few hidden or dynamic), editor-title and context menus for .clab.yml files, five keybindings, and about 34 settings. Heavy UI opens in webview panels: the TopoViewer (a canvas editor and viewer from the shared clab-ui package), inspect table, image manager, link-impairment table, Wireshark-over-VNC, welcome page and, only in the monorepo copy, a Settings page. Shells, SSH, Telnet, logs, fcli, SSHX/GoTTY sharing, Edgeshark install and draw.io graphs run containerlab or docker commands in VS Code terminals or the Output channel. The two copies are near-identical: the standalone repo (0.26.3, last commit 2026-09-17) is now a README redirect, and the monorepo copy (0.27.0) adds the Settings webview and four Appearance settings, node-level 'Capture Interfaces…' (multi-interface quick pick), SSH login from topology credentials and identity-file, 'Run with options…' lifecycle flags, the 'apply --dry-run' plan dialog, partial-destroy handling, the Podman-safe Edgeshark install, hiding dot-file labs, and the 'containerlab.settings.open' command with its gear in the view title. The package.json files differ in exactly: the captureInterfaces command, settings.open command, view/title menu, Appearance config block, port min/max, plus build metadata. Evidence paths use four prefixes, all relative to /tmp/claude-1000/-home-archtop-projects-clab-manager/6bfdb4f7-8b2f-4545-85c0-06d1bb42882d/scratchpad/upstream/: N/ = containerlab-app/apps/vscode-containerlab (new, 0.27.0), O/ = vscode-containerlab (old, 0.26.3), U/ = containerlab-app/packages/clab-ui/src (shared UI used by N; O consumes published @srl-labs/clab-ui 0.3.1, source not in the old repo), D/ = containerlab-app/docs. package.json line numbers are for N unless a separate O/ line is given. Nothing was run; all findings come from reading files.

## VS Code Containerlab extension (srl-labs/vscode-containerlab 0.26.3, plus the successor apps/vscode-containerlab 0.27.0 in srl-labs/containerlab-app)

The Containerlab extension integrates the containerlab CLI into VS Code for Linux and WSL hosts. Evidence paths in this inventory are relative to the scratchpad upstream/ directory. They use three prefixes: A is vscode-containerlab/src (0.26.3), B is containerlab-app/apps/vscode-containerlab/src (0.27.0), and U is containerlab-app/packages/clab-ui/src (the shared React UI that supplies the explorer, inspect, impairments, image manager, Wireshark, welcome, settings and TopoViewer webviews). The extension has no server and no login. Every action either runs the containerlab binary with child_process (deploy, destroy, redeploy, apply, start, stop, restart, save, graph, tools netem, tools sshx, tools gotty, events, inspect), calls the Docker or Podman API through dockerode (pause, unpause, port listing, image list, pull and remove, the Wireshark VNC container), or opens a named VS Code terminal that runs docker exec, docker logs, ssh or a fcli container. The UI has five surfaces. First is the Activity Bar explorer, a single React webview with Running Labs, Undeployed Local Labs and Help & Feedback sections, a wildcard or regex filter and grouped context menus. Second is TopoViewer, a React Flow canvas editor and viewer per lab, with the host side doing file watching, dirty-state detection, lifecycle streaming, custom node templates, icons and Grafana export. Third are the standalone webview panels: Inspect table, per-node Link Impairments table, Image Manager, Wireshark VNC, Welcome and (0.27.0 only) Settings. Fourth are command-palette, editor-title and keybinding entries for .clab.yml files. Fifth are a YAML completion provider for image: values and an Output channel. State is live: the explorer streams `containerlab events --format json --initial-state [--interface-stats]` and falls back to polling `containerlab inspect --all --details`. Capture is Edgeshark/packetflix (opens local Wireshark through a packetflix: URI) or a Wireshark-in-Docker container shown in an iframe; no raw tcpdump code exists in this source. Lab sharing is SSHX or GoTTY through `containerlab tools`. There is no config restore feature, only `containerlab save`. The 0.27.0 tree adds a Settings webview, appearance settings, a node-level multi-interface capture picker, SSH credentials taken from the topology file, a lifecycle options dialog, an apply dry-run plan dialog and Podman-friendly Edgeshark install.

## containerlab-app (server side: packages/app-server, packages/app-contract, packages/standalone-runtime)

containerlab-app is a pnpm monorepo (Node 24, TypeScript) whose web host is a Fastify backend-for-frontend (packages/app-server) plus a React renderer (packages/standalone-runtime) shipped as one Docker image (node:24.21.0-alpine, port 3001, non-root user node, HTTPS by default with an auto-generated self-signed cert). The BFF holds no Docker socket, no sudo and no lab files: every lab, node, file, image, capture and netem operation is forwarded as a REST/NDJSON/WebSocket call to a separate clab-api-server (default https://localhost:8090, runs as root or --privileged with docker.sock, /var/run/netns and the host user database) using a per-endpoint JWT. Authentication is delegated entirely to that API server: the browser posts Linux username and password once, the BFF exchanges them at the API server /login for a JWT of the chosen sessionDuration, keeps the token server-side in an in-memory session keyed by an httpOnly SameSite=strict cookie (clab_session, 30 days), and never returns tokens to the browser. One browser session can hold many endpoints (lab hosts); every proxy route resolves the endpoint from x-endpoint-id, ?endpointId, or the endpoint embedded in topologyId. The BFF exposes about 90 routes: auth and endpoint management (/auth/*), lab lifecycle with NDJSON streaming and reconciliation (/api/lab/*), runtime actions for nodes, terminals, netem, capture, images, sharing, fcli and draw.io (/api/runtime/*), a workspace file explorer with upload, download and zip or tar.gz archive building (/api/runtime/file-explorer/*, /files), a topology editing host protocol with server-side sessions (/api/topology/*), and three SSE streams plus two WebSocket proxies (terminal and Wireshark VNC). pyproject.toml and uv.lock are only for the documentation site (zensical), not the server. The standalone-runtime package is the browser-side adapter: endpoint profiles in localStorage, a live lab store fed by the events stream, an explorer tree with about 100 containerlab.* commands, lifecycle log streaming, Wireshark window launching, detached terminals, favorites, settings wiring and a desktop (Electron) title bar; most visible widgets themselves live in packages/clab-ui and are only wired here.

## @containerlab/clab-ui 0.4.0 and @containerlab/clab-viewer 0.1.0 (srl-labs/containerlab-app packages), compared with the clab-manager adapter pinned to clab-ui 0.3.2

The project is two npm packages from the srl-labs/containerlab-app monorepo. clab-ui (Apache-2.0, React 19, MUI, React Flow, Zustand, ELK, d3-force, maplibre, Monaco) is the shared topology editor: one App component plus a host contract, a host-side editing engine (TopologySessionCore) with a 21-verb command protocol, and several non-editor entrypoints (explorer, image manager, inspect, welcome, node impairments, wireshark VNC, workspace shell, settings) that only the VS Code extension and the standalone app mount. clab-viewer (MIT) bundles the same parser and renderers as a read-only canvas, an HTML custom element and an iframe message protocol. The editor is a floating navbar over a React Flow canvas with a docked context panel (Info, Edit, Nodes, Annotations, YAML, JSON tabs), context menus, keyboard shortcuts, lab settings dialog, bulk-link, SVG export and a large node editor (Basic, Kind, Components, Configuration, Runtime, Stages, Network, Advanced) with defaults/kinds/groups inheritance. Annotations (text, shapes, groups, traffic-rate) live in a sidecar JSON file; viewerSettings in it carry node style, link style, grid and label modes. The manager embeds the editor through clab-backup-ui/lab-builder/src/main.tsx: an in-memory two-file TopologySessionCore, window host with stubbed runtime methods, light theme, YAML and JSON tabs disabled, and two page modes (full lab builder, and the Edit map mode that accepts only 8 annotation-only commands plus a translated editNode for a device's look). The manager hides deploy, geo layout, split view and Grafana controls with CSS, patches nine spots of the 0.3.2 chunks, and replaces the YAML tab, drafts, templates, undo for the map and saving with its own pages. IMPORTANT VERSION SKEW: the source I read is 0.4.0 (shallow clone, no history); the manager ships 0.3.2. I probed the committed 0.3.2 bundle for strings and test ids: 0.4.0 added stages/wait-for, inheritance (Defaults and Groups), per-node credentials, kind-specific tab, boxed node style, link style elbow, rotation control, multi management networks, apply plan, run-with-options, capture-interfaces dialog, toolbar filter and hide-dummy/wait-for toggles, and removed the About modal and navbar lab name. Notes use this vocabulary: Manager EXPOSED (reachable in the lab builder through the unmodified editor UI), HIDDEN (display none in lab-builder.css), STUBBED (host method is a no-op in main.tsx), REPLACED (manager UI instead), REFUSED in map (not in MAP_COMMANDS), NOT MOUNTED (entrypoint the adapter never imports); 0.3.2 gives present, absent or n/v (not verifiable from minified strings). Evidence paths: clab-ui/src/... and clab-viewer/... are under upstream containerlab-app/packages/; manager: paths are under /home/archtop/projects/clab-manager/clab-backup-ui/.

## clab-manager (Containerlab Node Manager) 1.31.0

clab-manager is a browser workspace for students and instructors who run containerlab network labs on one Ubuntu 24.04 VM. It is a single FastAPI container (port 8081, host network, no Docker socket, no login) with a plain-JavaScript UI (no framework, no build step, no CDN) plus an embedded, prebuilt React/TypeScript topology editor (SR Labs @containerlab/clab-ui) used for the lab builder and for Edit map. The main surface is Home (My labs: Recent/All tabs, Deploy and Build cards) and a lab workspace with a constant header (state, devices ready, save chip, Save, Load, Lab actions) over four tabs: Topology, Devices, Tools, Advanced; Manager is a top-bar menu for VM connection, discovery, import, history, settings and Diagnostics. Standalone pages cover the browser SSH terminal (xterm.js), the CLI launcher, the lab builder, the map editor, the Wireshark viewer (noVNC), Diagnostics and two setup guides. Containerlab lifecycle actions (deploy, redeploy, start, stop, restart lab or one device, destroy, apply, inspect, save, create/delete topology files, clone, publish/revise from the builder) run only through reviewed, token-bound previews and one SSH account whose forced-command gateway maps exactly three request names (inspect, operations, git) to root sudo helpers; device work never uses helpers but goes over direct node SSH (Ansible network_cli for backups/login checks, Paramiko for terminals, readiness, Load and network-design apply). Device support is cEOS, cJunosEvolved, vJunos-switch, vQFX (backup only) and XRv9k with documented default logins; Load (replace running configuration with the NOS's own timed recovery) is proven only for cEOS, cJunosEvolved, vJunos-switch and XRv9k; any other SSH node gets a terminal only. Progress saving is Git based (Save commits the whole lab on the VM, Upload is the only thing that pushes, checkpoints, starting point, course lab states, All versions, Load with Undo), plus an experimental netlab-based Network design tab that generates and can apply per-device configuration. Packet capture is a separate side stack (Edgeshark discovery, a session service that alone holds the Docker socket, one Wireshark container per session, max 4 sessions) relayed through the manager same-origin. Telemetry and Grafana are retired (only the removal of leftover device configuration lines remains); there is no host CPU/memory metrics feature. Operator-facing surfaces include a guided plain/full-screen installer, a check-install.sh health report, setup scripts and a scaffold tool.

## clab-manager (Containerlab Node Manager) 1.31.0

clab-manager is a single-process FastAPI service (plus a plain-JavaScript browser UI, no framework, no build step) for a lab VM that runs containerlab. It has no login: access is by network placement, a same-origin guard on /api, a Host-name check and per-WebSocket Origin checks. It reaches the VM only through one SSH account whose forced command (deploy/clab-manager-gateway) maps to three root-owned sudo helpers: inspect/file reading (host_files.py), containerlab lifecycle and file writing (host_operations.py) and owner-scoped Git (host_git.py). It reaches the lab devices directly over SSH (Ansible network_cli for backups, Paramiko for terminals, login checks, restores and design applies). The UI surfaces are: index.html (Home "My labs", then a lab page with Topology, Devices, Tools and Advanced tabs, the Save chip and Load panel in the lab header, many dialogs and drawers), terminal.html (xterm SSH), workspace.html (CLI launcher), debug.html (Diagnostics), vm-connection.html and capture-setup.html (admin guides), capture-session.html (browser Wireshark over noVNC), lab-builder.html and map-editor.html (the embedded SR Labs clab-ui editor, committed as a built bundle, with manager-owned pages around it). Functionally it covers: lab discovery/import, reviewed containerlab operations (deploy, redeploy, destroy, apply, start, stop, restart, restart one node, save, inspect, create/delete/clone/publish/revise files), a topology map with annotations and draw.io export, device login/readiness tracking, configuration backups (Junos, EOS, IOS XR) with scheduling and ZIP/per-device download, Git-backed Save/Load with transactional whole-configuration replacement and timed rollback, browser Wireshark capture via Edgeshark, a visual lab builder with browser drafts, and an experimental netlab-based Network design generator/applier. Supported NOS kinds for backup, login defaults and restore are only juniper_cjunosevolved, juniper_vjunosswitch, juniper_vqfx, cisco_xrv9k and arista_ceos; everything else is a Linux host or unmanaged. There is no dark mode or theme switch, no link-impairment, interface-statistics, container-log, exec or containerlab tools UI (the editor adapter stubs several of those upstream editor hooks). The retired telemetry/Grafana stack survives only as a one-time cleanup dialog.
